### R-USSHORT-BATCH5-VALIDATE-STATUS-RECORD-NONBOOL-FLAG-VALUE-GAP - validate_status_record accepts int 0/1 as a flag value (frozenset bool-equality) and raises a bare TypeError on unhashable value/coverage — contained today, not a bypass

- **Status**: **resolved** (Claude re-review PASS 2026-07-03; OFFLINE; committed this cycle). `_bool_or_none` is `x is True or x is False or x is None` (IDENTITY), so the added `elif not _bool_or_none(value): return False` rejects int `0`/`1` (which hash-collide with `False`/`True` in the `_VALID_*` tuples — `(1,"observed") in _VALID_DELISTED` is genuinely True) AND unhashable `[]`/`{}` BEFORE any membership; the new `isinstance(coverage, str)` guard (before the first `coverage in {...}`) + bankruptcy strict `value is True/False` + `isinstance(screen_status, str)` close the coverage/screen_status legs. Whole class covered (value + coverage + screen_status across all 4 flags). Reviewer probe: all 4 flags × {0,1,1.0,"True",[],{}} on value + {[],{},1,None} on coverage + hostile int-subclass(==1) → `False` with ZERO bare `TypeError`; a valid resolver record still validates True (no over-reject). Independent §3.5 agent (read-only current tree): whole-class HELD (every JSON-constructible hostile input → False, guard order correct, no membership runs on unguarded input), reverse over-reject HELD (all 13 real resolver records + 100% of the four `_VALID_*` tables still validate True). Reviewer re-ran full offline `*us_short*` `3518 OK` (zero regression). NB the historical "`_bool_or_none(0/1) also returns True`" line below is a Was-record inaccuracy (it returns False via identity); the int passed via the `_VALID_*` tuple equality, which the fix now pre-empts. (Was: open — Codex repair pending; §3.5 re-scan found by the independent agent on injection `72f20fab`; contained validator-robustness, not a fail-open; 教训⑰.)
- **Severity**: **P3** (validator-contract robustness; contained end-to-end today by `is`-identity checks + the candidate schema, but violates the closed-world / fail-closed contract and is one refactor from a leak).
- **Scope reviewed**: `engine/us_short_status_source.py::validate_status_record` + `_bool_or_none` + the `_VALID_*` frozenset tuples (slice-1a engine consumed by injection `72f20fab` + candidate schema v1.1.0 + the `bf95b35` run_fetch wiring). Independent §3.5 adversarial agent (~20 attack classes) + reviewer's own forgery probe (launder halt / strip provenance — both rejected).
- **Verified correct (NOT findings — the CORE holds)**: no eligibility BYPASS on ~20 forgery/laundering/identity/clock/schema-gap attacks. Two independent re-derivations (verdict-from-stored-flags via `cheap_eligible`; flags-from-provenance via `status_flags_for_row`) + ticker-identity binding + closed-world `_VALID_*` tables form a complete lattice. Reviewer's own probe: laundering a halted ticker to `eligible=True` (provenance still halted) → `RuntimeError: status halted disagrees with stored status_provenance`; stripping provenance from a sourced row → schema `required` ValidationError. Schema `if/then`+`const false` is load-bearing; `validate_status_record` is a strict superset of the schema on the flag-tuple contract (no shape validated by neither).
- **Finding 1 — int `0`/`1` accepted as a flag value**: `validate_status_record` tests `(value, coverage) in _VALID_DELISTED` (etc.); in Python `(1,"observed") == (True,"observed")` with equal hash, so `value ∈ {0,1}` (int) passes the bool-domain tuple; `_bool_or_none(0/1)` also returns True. Contract is "a record the resolver could ACTUALLY emit" (bool only). Contained: `status_flags_for_row` uses `value is True`/`is False` (identity) → an int is OMITTED (conservative-reject → `cheap_eligible` rejects) or erased to False (bankruptcy positive-detection-only); the candidate schema rejects int in stored provenance (`type: boolean|null`). No live leak today, but one `is`→truthiness refactor away, and it contradicts the module's closed-world claim. `engine/us_short_status_source.py:566/569/573/578` + `_bool_or_none:505`.
- **Finding 2 — bare `TypeError` on unhashable value/coverage**: `record["flags"][flag]["value"] = {}` (or `[]`) → `(value, coverage) not in _VALID_*` → uncaught `TypeError: unhashable type` instead of the module's fail-closed `StatusSourceError`/`False`. Reachable via the documented fixture entry `apply_pass1(status_records=...)` → `status_flags_for_row` → `validate_status_record` (crashes with a stack trace, not a controlled rejection). On the artifact-validation path it is pre-empted by the schema (`boolean|null`), so no fail-open; but it breaks the function's typed / fail-closed contract. Same root as Finding 1.
- **Why material**: both violate `validate_status_record`'s own closed-world / fail-closed contract and match recorded 教训⑰ ("enum `x in frozenset`: isinstance-guard before consuming unvalidated external values; unhashable list/dict throws a bare TypeError; align to the reference cut's safety semantics"). The sibling `bankruptcy`/`otc` resolvers DO have strict `isinstance` guards; `validate_status_record`'s tuple checks are the one place that skipped it. The original injection 审查 did not probe the validator with int/unhashable flag values.
- **Required repair (Optional-recommended, offline)**: add a strict `type(value) is bool` (+ `isinstance(coverage, str)`) guard BEFORE the `_VALID_*` membership in `validate_status_record`, and fix `_bool_or_none` to reject int, so a non-bool / unhashable flag value returns `False` (fail-closed) rather than passing (int) or raising a bare `TypeError`. Tests: `value ∈ {0, 1, 1.0, "True", [], {}}` per flag → `validate_status_record=False`. Do not change the resolver, eligibility contract, or schema. Can be bundled with `R-USSHORT-BATCH5-HALT-FEED-PARSE-SILENT-DROP-FAILOPEN` status-source hardening.
- **Verification evidence**: independent §3.5 agent reproduced both (int `value=1` → `validate_status_record=True`; `value={}` → `TypeError`); reviewer forgery probe confirmed the core forgery defense holds (launder + strip both rejected). No provider/live/network path.
- **Codex repair (2026-07-03; OFFLINE; pending Claude re-review)**: `validate_status_record` now rejects non-string `coverage` before any set membership, rejects non-strict conservative flag `value` unless it is exactly `True` / `False` / `None`, and rejects bankruptcy `value` / `screen_status` unless they are strict bool / str before `_VALID_*` membership. Added focused tests proving `value in {0, 1, 1.0, "True", [], {}}`, non-string coverage, and non-string bankruptcy screen_status return `False` instead of passing or raising a bare `TypeError`. No resolver, eligibility, schema, provider/live, DataHub, production, broker/order, or cross-lane change.

### R-USSHORT-BATCH5-HALT-FEED-PARSE-SILENT-DROP-FAILOPEN - the Nasdaq halt-feed parser silently drops unparseable items, so an observed-but-incompletely-parsed feed marks genuinely-halted tickers as not-halted (clean) and admits them

- **Status**: **resolved** (Claude re-review PASS 2026-07-03; OFFLINE; committed this cycle). `parse_halt_symbols_from_rss` now counts symbol-less `<item>`s and raises `RuntimeError`; `run_fetch`'s existing `except Exception` around `fetch_nasdaq_trade_halt_feed` catches it → `halt_feed={"observed":False}` / `halt_feed_state="down"` → `_resolve_halted` unknown → `cheap_eligible` rejects (verified END-TO-END by the §3.5 agent: a genuinely-halted ticker becomes INELIGIBLE, not clean-admitted). Latent-3 closed: `_assert_text_safe` now forbids `nasdaqtrader.com`. Reviewer probe: symbol-less item → RuntimeError, partial (good+bad) → RuntimeError, happy → symbols, empty (0-item) → `[]` (legit no-halts day, not fail-open — absence from an observed complete feed is real not-halted evidence). Independent §3.5 agent (read-only current tree): the hardening's invariant HELD end-to-end + surfaced ONE PRE-EXISTING latent residual in the UNTOUCHED `_symbol_from_halt_item` regex fallback → recorded as Optional `R-USSHORT-BATCH5-HALT-SYMBOL-WRONG-CANONICAL-MISATTRIB-LATENT`. Optional Latent-2 (`_status_as_of` ascii) not taken. Reviewer re-ran full offline `*us_short*` `3518 OK` (zero regression). (Was: open — Codex repair pending; latent P2 fail-open code path found on `bf95b35`; my P1 overstatement was corrected to latent by the §3.5 agent — the real feed carries authoritative `<IssueSymbol>` checked first.)
- **Severity**: **P2** (latent fail-open / robustness: with the current real feed the primary `<IssueSymbol>` path screens correctly; a feed-format change or an unparseable item would silently miss halts with NO detection signal. Fix before the status screen is promoted).
- **Scope reviewed**: `runners/us_short_universe_fetch.py::parse_halt_symbols_from_rss` / `_symbol_from_halt_item` / `fetch_nasdaq_trade_halt_feed` / `build_live_status_records` (this slice `bf95b35`), and `engine/us_short_status_source.py::_resolve_halted` (slice-1a). Read-only adjacency: `status_flags_for_row`, `classify_status_source_outcomes`, `apply_pass1`.
- **Technical finding**: `_resolve_halted` treats an OBSERVED halt feed as the COMPLETE current-halt list — `ticker in halted_set → halted=True`; `ticker NOT in halted_set → halted=False` (coverage `observed`, i.e. screened clean). `halted_set` comes from `parse_halt_symbols_from_rss`, which builds `{symbol for item in items if (symbol := _symbol_from_halt_item(item))}` — **items whose symbol can't be extracted are SILENTLY DROPPED**. `_symbol_from_halt_item` tries `<IssueSymbol>` → regex `Symbol:...` in title/description → bare-title fallback → `canonical_us_ticker`; if none yields a canonical ticker it returns `None` and the item vanishes. So if the Nasdaq RSS shape changes (e.g. drops/renames `IssueSymbol`) or contains any item the parser can't map, that halt is missed, the feed is still `observed=True` with a partial/empty `halted_set`, and every un-extracted halted ticker resolves to `halted=False`/`coverage=observed` → admitted as clean. NO detection: `halt_feed_state="ok"`, `classify_status_source_outcomes` sees `exchange_halt_feed="ok"` → `block_or_no_emit=False`.
- **Reproduced (offline probe, no network)**: a 2-item RSS with real halts but no `<IssueSymbol>` → `parse_halt_symbols_from_rss == []`; a genuinely-halted `AAPL` → `resolve_status_record` flag `halted.value=False`, `coverage="observed"`; `status_flags_for_row` → `{halted: False}` = admitted; `classify_status_source_outcomes({ticker_reference:ok, exchange_halt_feed:ok, sec_8k:missing})` → `block_or_no_emit=False`. An all-items-unparseable feed silently disables halt screening for the ENTIRE universe with no signal.
- **Why material**: this is the precise fail-open class the parent finding exists to prevent — unknown/unobserved status silently treated as clean. A FETCH failure fails CLOSED (`observed=False` → `halted=None` unknown → conservative reject); a PARSE gap on a successfully-fetched feed fails OPEN. A short system admitting a halted stock is a real safety defect, and a feed-format change would systematically disable halt screening with no signal. My original 审查 did not read `_resolve_halted` / the parser closely enough and passed it — this is exactly the rigor gap the user flagged.
- **Required repair** (offline; no live call): make the halt-feed parse fail-closed on incompleteness. `parse_halt_symbols_from_rss` must not silently drop items — track items it cannot map to a canonical symbol; if any item is unparseable (or items are present but zero symbols extracted, or a per-item required field such as `IssueSymbol` is absent), treat the feed observation as UNRELIABLE: surface `exchange_halt_feed` as `degraded`/`down` to `classify_status_source_outcomes` (so `_resolve_halted` returns `halted=None` unknown → conservative reject, and the all-critical/no-emit logic can act), or otherwise mark the halt coverage unusable rather than observed-clean. Add tests: a halt item missing `IssueSymbol` / unparseable → the affected ticker is NOT clean-admitted (halted unknown or feed degraded); an all-unparseable feed does not silently pass every ticker; a well-formed feed positive control still screens correctly. Keep offline; no live/network.
- **Verification evidence (this re-review)**: offline python-stdin probe reproduced the latent fail-open (no-`IssueSymbol` halt items → `parse_halt_symbols_from_rss==[]` → AAPL `halted=False/coverage=observed` → admitted); full read of `run_fetch` + `_resolve_halted` / `_resolve_delisted_and_otc` / `status_flags_for_row` / `validate_status_record` / `classify_status_source_outcomes` (the engine bodies the original PASS assumed rather than read); delisted/otc/bankruptcy paths re-checked and are fail-closed or by-design.
- **Independent §3.5 adversarial agent (completed 2026-07-03; throwaway `bf95b35` worktree; no network)**: could NOT find a reproducible live fail-open. It independently ran ~13 attack classes and confirmed the core is fail-closed at multiple layers — unknown→reject (`cheap_eligible` strict isinstance-bool), halt-down→reject, all-critical→no-emit before any write, forgery→rejected by `validate_status_record` (12 mutants) + per-row provenance re-derivation + schema `if/then`+`const false` (8 mutants), record↔row identity binding, clock/boundary. It independently found THIS same halt-parse gap and rated it latent (real feed uses `IssueSymbol`). It ALSO surfaced two gaps the reviewer missed, both non-bypass: **(Latent-2, P3)** `_status_as_of_from_decision_date` accepts fullwidth-digit / month-13 `decision_date` (`"２０２６０６３０"` / `"20261340"`) because it uses `str.isdigit()` without the `.isascii()`+`strptime`/calendar guard its sibling `_valid_as_of` has — caught downstream (`resolve_status_record` raises) and `decision_date` comes from the frozen calendar, so no bypass, but it violates the project-wide ascii-clock convention. **(Latent-3, P2 hygiene)** the tracked-summary secret/URL scanner `_assert_text_safe` forbids `api.massive.com`/`data.sec.gov`/`www.sec.gov`/`financialmodelingprep.com`/`apikey=` but NOT `nasdaqtrader.com` — the provider domain THIS commit introduces; nothing routes that URL/body into the tracked summary today (halt payload → gitignored raw only), so no active leak, but the belt-and-suspenders provider-URL guard has a hole for the exact provider added here. **Required (Latent-3)**: add `nasdaqtrader.com` to the scanner fragment list. **Optional (Latent-2)**: mirror `.isascii()`+real-date validation in `_status_as_of_from_decision_date`.
- **Codex repair (2026-07-03; OFFLINE; pending Claude re-review)**: `parse_halt_symbols_from_rss` now counts per-item parse failures and raises if any RSS item cannot be mapped to a canonical ticker; the existing `run_fetch` exception path then marks `exchange_halt_feed` down so `_resolve_halted` emits unknown rather than observed-clean. Added positive and partial/all-unparseable RSS tests plus a run_fetch down-path regression. `_assert_text_safe` now also rejects `nasdaqtrader.com`, with a focused summary-scan test. Optional Latent-2 was not taken. No live/network/provider call, bankruptcy 8-K scan, Pass2 wiring, DataHub, production, broker/order, or cross-lane change.
- **Honesty note**: the original 审查 (this reviewer) MISSED all three gaps (rigor gap the user flagged). On the re-review the reviewer then OVERSTATED Latent-1 as an active P1; the independent §3.5 agent both corroborated the misses and corrected the overstatement. Recorded because the reviewer-bias correction is the point of the independent pass.

### R-USSHORT-BATCH5-HALT-SYMBOL-WRONG-CANONICAL-MISATTRIB-LATENT - `_symbol_from_halt_item`'s title/description regex fallback can map a halt to a wrong-but-canonical symbol, silently missing the real halt without tripping the new unparseable counter

- **Status**: **resolved** (Claude re-review PASS 2026-07-03; OFFLINE; committed this cycle). `_symbol_from_halt_item` now requires a non-empty authoritative `<IssueSymbol>` (`_xml_child_text` strips → blank/missing → `None`) and drops the title/description `Symbol:`/bare-title regex fallback (+ the `re` import), so a symbol-less item feeds the existing unparseable→feed-down→unknown→reject path instead of guessing a wrong-but-canonical ticker. Reviewer reverse-probe: IssueSymbol present (plain/lower/whitespace) → correct canonical; blank/missing IssueSymbol + `Symbol: XYZ`/bare-title → `RuntimeError` (fail-closed); the agent's GME-mapped-to-XYZ misattribution repro now RAISES (closed, not papered). No existing positive test relied on the dropped fallback (grep `Symbol:` = only the new red-green test; it was dead code) → nothing over-rejected offline. Reviewer ran full offline `*us_short*` `3520 OK` (zero regression). Gated residual (SR-PROVIDER-001, SAFE direction): if the LIVE feed ever carries a legit non-`IssueSymbol` item it now marks the feed down (over-reject) rather than admit — conservative, detectable via `critical_failed`. (Was: open Optional latent, surfaced by the §3.5 agent during the halt-parse-hardening re-review; Codex repair 2026-07-03.)
- **Severity**: **P3** (latent fail-open, DEAD for the documented Nasdaq feed: `_symbol_from_halt_item` checks the authoritative `<ndaq:IssueSymbol>` FIRST — see `test_parse_halt_symbols_prefers_namespaced_issue_symbol` — so the title/description `Symbol:` regex fallback only bites when IssueSymbol is absent/empty AND the free text canonicalizes to a DIFFERENT valid ticker, not the real feed shape).
- **Technical finding**: the new `parse_halt_symbols_from_rss` unparseable-counter only counts items that extract to `None`. An item that extracts to a WRONG-but-canonical symbol is not "unparseable", so it does not trip the counter. Agent repro: a genuine GME halt with NO `<IssueSymbol>` + description `"…Symbol: XYZ…"` → parses to `['XYZ']`, GME silently missing, no raise → GME admits clean. Contained because the real feed's authoritative `<IssueSymbol>` is checked first, making the regex fallback dead.
- **Optional repair** (offline; no live call): in `_symbol_from_halt_item`, drop the title/description `Symbol:` regex fallback, OR require `<IssueSymbol>` and count its absence as unparseable (feeding the existing fail-closed path). No resolver/eligibility/schema/provider/live change.
- **Verification evidence**: independent §3.5 agent reproduced (`Symbol: XYZ` free-text → `['XYZ']`, real GME halt missing, no raise) and confirmed the IssueSymbol-first path makes it dead for the documented feed. No provider/live/network path.
- **Codex repair (2026-07-03; OFFLINE; pending Claude re-review)**: `_symbol_from_halt_item` now requires a non-empty `<IssueSymbol>` and returns `None` otherwise, feeding the existing `parse_halt_symbols_from_rss` unparseable counter. Added red-green tests for a free-text `Symbol: XYZ` item with no `IssueSymbol` and a blank `IssueSymbol` title-fallback item; both now raise `RuntimeError` instead of producing a wrong canonical symbol. No resolver/eligibility/schema/provider/live/DataHub/production/broker/order/cross-lane change.

### R-USSHORT-BATCH5-SEAM-MOMENTUM-CONTRACT-AND-COVERAGE-CONSISTENCY-GAP - momentum projection seam trusts contradictory coverage and has no frozen cross-module contract

- **Status**: **resolved** (Codex independent full re-review PASS 2026-07-02; OFFLINE; commit pending in this review cycle) after Claude residual-2 `修复` (2026-07-02; +1 independent adversarial pass — 1 HIGH found & closed) after **open** (Codex independent full re-review FAIL 2026-07-02; residual coverage-margin + public-boundary classes) after Claude round-1 `修复` after **open** (Codex clean-slate review FAIL 2026-07-02; US-short batch5 Cut 6-a only).
- **Severity**: **P1** before this seam is consumed by the batch4 score composer or any later Cut 6 assembly/wiring.
- **Scope reviewed**: new untracked `engine/us_short_seam_momentum.py` and `tests/test_us_short_seam_momentum.py`; read-only adjacency covered `engine/us_short_momentum.py`, `engine/us_short_core_score.py`, the weekend-analysis consumer, and `docs/us_short_system_design.md` §4.0/§4.2/§18.2/§19. The already committed Cut 5 probe and Cut 4/Cut 5 producers, Cut 6-b~6-g, provider/live/DataHub/production/ship-gate, broker/order paths, A-share, A-long, and US-long were excluded.
- **Technical finding A - the cross-module seam is not schema-first**: `project_momentum_block` consumes the batch5 producer result and emits score-composer input, but there is no schema, frozen binding artifact, mutation test, or route pointer for either side of this new shared contract. The implementation selectively trusts `momentum_block`, `insufficient_history`, and `insufficient_coverage` while ignoring the producer's `coverage_matrix`, `min_coverage`, and `sub_feature_coverage`. This violates the owner rule in §18.2 that a shared contract must be frozen schema-first before cross-module consumption.
- **Technical finding B - contradictory coverage can be forged into a scored contribution**: an actual producer output was mutated so `momentum_block['AAPL']=50` while `coverage_matrix['AAPL']={'n_present':0,'scored':false}`. The seam accepted it and emitted AAPL as scored `50` rather than rejecting the contradiction or neutral-filling it. Missing/extra matrix identities and disagreement among `n_present`, `min_coverage`, the two insufficient sets, and the scored set are likewise outside the seam's validation boundary. A caller can therefore present score-ready values that the producer's own coverage evidence says are unscored.
- **Technical finding C - malformed identities/numerics do not consistently fail inside the domain boundary**: canonical duplicates inside an insufficient list, e.g. `['AAPL', 'aapl']`, are silently collapsed by set construction rather than rejected as ambiguous producer output. `_finite_block_value` accepts numeric subclasses and calls `float(value)` outside a protected conversion boundary; a hostile float subclass whose `__float__` raises leaks raw `RuntimeError` instead of `MomentumSeamError`. Existing hostile comparison tests do not cover this dispatch path.
- **Why material**: this seam decides whether the 40% momentum component contributes a real score or is omitted for the core-score neutral default. Trusting a score that contradicts the producer's coverage record can turn unavailable evidence into a valid ranking contribution. Without a frozen contract, later Cut 6 composition can bind to an incomplete shape while tests remain green. The duplicate and raw-exception paths also make malformed producer output non-deterministic at a public module boundary.
- **Required repair**: keep the repair offline and confined to Cut 6-a. (1) Add a schema-first seam contract: binding artifact, JSON schema, adversarial schema mutations, engine/binding triangulation, and a thin `docs/README.md` route pointer. Freeze the accepted producer input shape and projection output/disposition shape, including the load-bearing coverage policy; do not add transient review state. (2) Validate coverage as one coherent partition: canonical ticker identities in `coverage_matrix` must exactly equal the union of scored/history/coverage identities; each matrix row must have the exact typed shape; `scored` must agree with `n_present >= min_coverage`; history must mean `n_present == 0`; insufficient coverage must mean `0 < n_present < min_coverage`; reject missing, extra, or contradictory identities. If `sub_feature_coverage` remains in the accepted producer contract, freeze and validate its ticker universe and relation rather than silently ignoring it. (3) Reject post-canonical duplicates within every disposition list; do not silently set-collapse them. (4) Keep malformed numeric values inside `MomentumSeamError`, preferably by accepting only exact built-in numeric types or by safely containing conversion exceptions; add hostile `__float__`/numeric-subclass tests. (5) Add a narrow end-to-end projection-to-`core_score`/composer test proving a valid scored value contributes and history/coverage/absent dispositions omit the block and receive the documented neutral default. Do not implement Cut 6-b~6-g, fetch data, wire provider/live/DataHub/production, change scoring weights, or cross market lanes.
- **Review verification evidence**: focused `python -m unittest tests.test_us_short_seam_momentum` ran `34 OK`; full offline `python -m unittest discover -s tests -p 'test_us_short*.py'` ran `3378 OK` with one environment-caused Windows symlink skip; route/doc guards ran `52 OK`; `py_compile` passed. Direct probes used the real momentum producer shape and reproduced contradictory coverage acceptance, canonical duplicate collapse, and raw hostile-`__float__` exception leakage; a valid neutral omission still produced the core-score neutral value `50`. No provider/live/network/DataHub/production path ran.
- **Optional findings**: none.
- **Repair — findings A/B/C + Required 5-point (Claude 2026-07-02; OFFLINE; fixed pending Codex; +1 independent adversarial pass)** — closes all three registered classes, confined to Cut 6-a offline; the class was enumerated from the Required-repair TEXT, not the demonstrated probes. **A (schema-first cross-module contract)**: new frozen binding `docs/us_short_seam_momentum_binding_20260702.json` + schema `schemas/us_short_seam_momentum_binding.schema.json` (Draft-07; const-pins BOTH sides — accepted producer-result key set, block value domain, numeric policy, sub-feature universe, coverage row shape, coverage partition policy, disposition vocabulary, projection output shape, producer ref, all-gated authorization boundary; `additionalProperties:false`) + adversarial schema-mutation test (16 mutants) + engine consts triangulated `== load_binding()` and `_SUB_FEATURE_UNIVERSE == momentum_block`'s own default (conformance) + thin `docs/README.md` route row. **B (coverage as one coherent partition)**: `_validate_producer_result` now consumes the previously-ignored `coverage_matrix` / `min_coverage` / `sub_feature_coverage` and rejects every contradiction — matrix canonical identities must EXACTLY equal `scored ∪ history ∪ coverage` (no missing/extra/duplicate); each row is exactly `{n_present:int, scored:bool}`; `scored == (n_present >= min_coverage)`; `history ⟺ n_present == 0`; `insufficient_coverage ⟺ 0 < n_present < min_coverage`; `sub_feature_coverage` keys == the frozen universe with counts exact-int ∈ [0, #identities]; `n_present` / `min_coverage` additionally bounded ≤ `len(sub_feature_universe)` (adversarial-pass hardening). The exact forged-coverage probe (`momentum_block['AAPL']=50` with `coverage_matrix['AAPL']={n_present:0,scored:false}`) now raises `MomentumSeamError`. **C (identity + numeric, whole-class)**: every identity-bearing collection (momentum_block keys, coverage_matrix keys, insufficient_history, insufficient_coverage) rejects a post-canonical duplicate (no silent set-collapse); `_finite_block_value` now accepts ONLY an exact built-in `int`/`float` (bool, numeric-string, numpy-or-other numeric subclass, and any hostile `__float__`/`__le__`/`__ge__` are rejected BEFORE any conversion/comparison dispatch) finite and in [0,100] — one structural guard closes the whole numeric-subclass class, incl. the raw `__float__` exception leak that the earlier `float()`-first fix had left. **(5) end-to-end**: `EndToEndCoreScoreTests` proves a scored value contributes to `core_score` and a history/coverage/absent disposition omits the block so `core_score` applies NEUTRAL_BLOCK 50 + `missing_blocks`. **Class coverage (from Required text)**: {binding + schema + mutation + triangulation + route} ∪ {coverage partition: identity-union + row-shape + scored/n_present/min_coverage relation + per-disposition band + sub-feature universe + physical upper bound} ∪ {post-canonical duplicate in EVERY collection} ∪ {exact-builtin-numeric closing `__float__`/`__le__`/subclass} ∪ {end-to-end neutral default}. **§3.5 independent adversarial pass** (fail-closed/validation boundary): a cold-attack agent (not shown the fix, told to derive the invariant set itself and attack the whole artifact) ran 117 attacks / 26 groups → 0 bypass, 0 raw-exception leak, 0 input mutation; its one LOW (missing n_present/min_coverage physical upper bound) was closed this round rather than disclosed-and-deferred. Boundary held: no provider/live/network/fetch/DataHub/production/ship-gate, no Cut 6-b~6-g, no scoring-weight change, no A-share/A-long/US-long. Verify: focused unit `55 OK` + schema `16 OK`; the three Codex probes (forged coverage / canonical duplicate / hostile `__float__`) each now raise `MomentumSeamError`; full offline `*us_short*` `3415 OK`; route/doc guards `52 OK`; `py_compile` OK; new files LF / no-BOM / JSON-parse OK; `git diff --check` CRLF-only.
- **Codex independent full re-review residual (2026-07-02; FAIL; Cut 6-a only)**: the three previously demonstrated probes are closed, but the claimed whole-class closure is incomplete in two material classes. **Residual A — `coverage_matrix` and `sub_feature_coverage` are still independent, forgeable summaries rather than one coherent producer fact.** The real producer derives row degrees (`coverage_matrix[*].n_present`) and column degrees (`sub_feature_coverage[*]`) from the same ticker-by-feature incidence matrix, so at minimum their totals must be equal and the two degree sequences must be jointly realizable as a binary incidence matrix. The seam checks only each individual bound. A forged one-ticker result with `n_present=4`, `scored=true`, and all seven sub-feature counts `0` passes and projects AAPL as scored `50`; an attack sweep accepted seven unequal-total combinations. Even the test helper labelled `COHERENT` creates impossible positives: for a scored ticker it writes `n_present=min_coverage=4` while setting all seven sub-feature counts to `1` (row total `4`, column total `7`). Equal totals alone are also insufficient: row degrees `[7,1]` with feature counts `[2,2,2,2,0,0,0]` have equal total `8` but cannot correspond to any binary ticker-by-feature matrix. The binding's `sub_feature_relation` freezes only key identity and per-count range, so the omitted conservation/realisability rule is absent from both runtime and schema-first contract. The schema also leaves `identity_policy` as arbitrary `type:string` and makes the load-bearing `neutral_fill_note` optional/free text; same-shape policy drift can therefore validate despite the artifact claiming these semantics are frozen. **Residual B — the public fail-closed boundary still leaks attacker-controlled dunder exceptions outside `MomentumSeamError`.** The module accepts container subclasses with `isinstance` and dispatches `.keys()`, `.items()`, or iteration before containment; direct attacks against top result keys, block items, insufficient-list iteration, matrix items, sub-feature items, and target iteration each leaked raw `RuntimeError`. A plain dict with a hashable extra key whose `__str__` raises also leaks through the error diagnostic. This contradicts the public docstring/exception contract and the repair claim of zero raw-exception leakage. **Required residual repair**: keep the repair offline and Cut 6-a-only. (1) Freeze and enforce the complete available coverage relation: row-degree total equals column-degree total and the row/column degree sequences are bipartite-realizable (or change the accepted producer shape to carry exact per-ticker present-sub-feature identities and validate those directly); add unequal-total and equal-total-impossible mutants plus real-producer positive controls. Rebuild `_result` fixtures from a realizable incidence matrix instead of independently inventing margins. (2) Const-pin/structure every load-bearing binding policy, including the exact identity authority and neutral-fill-to-composer omission rule; require them in schema and triangulate them to engine behavior, with planted same-shape mutations. (3) Close the hostile public-input class before dispatch: require exact built-in container/key types where the real producer emits them or contain all access/diagnostic exceptions as `MomentumSeamError`; test every public container/diagnostic surface, not only numeric and ticker subclasses. Do not implement Cut 6-b~6-g, change scoring weights, fetch/provider/live/DataHub/production, or cross market lanes. **Review evidence**: focused runtime `55 OK`; current bundled interpreter lacks `jsonschema`, so the new schema suite produced `16` dependency errors and no clean schema PASS is claimed; full offline discovery ran `3415` tests with the same `6` unrelated batch4 context-builder subprocess failures caused by missing child-process schema validation plus `1` Windows symlink skip; route/doc guards `52 OK`; `py_compile`, JSON parse, no-BOM, and `git diff --check` passed. Real producer output projects correctly and input immutability held. Direct adversarial probes reproduced all residuals above; no provider/live/network/DataHub/production path ran.
- **Repair — residual A/B (Claude 2026-07-02; OFFLINE; fixed pending Codex; +1 independent adversarial pass)** — closes both round-2 residual classes, confined to Cut 6-a offline. **Residual A (coverage margins are ONE producer fact, not two forgeable summaries)**: `_validate_producer_result` now enforces cross-summary coherence via `_assert_coverage_realizable` — Σ `coverage_matrix.n_present` (row degrees) == Σ `sub_feature_coverage` counts (column degrees) AND the two degree sequences must be jointly realizable as a binary ticker×sub-feature incidence matrix (Gale-Ryser: rows sorted desc, ∀k Σ top-k rows ≤ Σ_j min(col_j, k)). A result inventing the two margins independently is rejected whether totals are unequal (`n_present=4` + all-zero sub-feature counts) or equal-but-unrealizable (rows `[7,1]` / cols `[2,2,2,2,0,0,0]`). binding+schema add const-pinned `margin_conservation` + `margin_realizability`; the free-text load-bearing `identity_policy` + `neutral_fill_note` are now const-pinned (neutral_fill_note required) and the identity authority is triangulated to the engine (`sm.canonical_us_ticker is` the eligibility-gate policy). The `_result` test fixtures are REBUILT from a real incidence (scored ticker holds exactly min_coverage sub-features, insufficient_coverage 1, history 0) so they are conserved+realizable by construction; a `RealProducerPositiveControlTests` runs the actual `momentum_block` and projects it (no false-negative). **Residual B (public boundary leaked attacker-controlled dunder exceptions)**: every container must be an EXACT built-in type (`type(x) is dict`/`list`, tuple allowed for targets) checked BEFORE any `.keys()`/`.items()`/iteration (a subclass overriding access is rejected fail-closed); every dict key is validated `type(k) is str` before any set/`in`/format op (top result, momentum_block, coverage_matrix + each row, sub_feature_coverage); and the extra-key diagnostic reports only the known-safe missing set + a COUNT of illegal keys, never formatting an untrusted key. **§3.5 independent adversarial pass** (≥2-FAIL finding + fail-closed boundary; cold-attack agent, not shown the fix, told the producer derives both margins from one incidence): verified Gale-Ryser against 672,646 exhaustive + ~66,000 random/large brute-force pairs (0 mismatch) and 1,680 real producer outputs (no false-negative), and the full container/key/diagnostic battery is contained — and FOUND 1 HIGH the two prior reviews + my self-review missed: a legitimate huge exact int (`10**309`) block value overflowed `float(x)` with a raw `OverflowError`. **Closed this round** (`_finite_block_value` contains `OverflowError` → None; +2 tests) rather than disclosed-and-deferred. **Class coverage (from residual text)**: {row/col conservation + Gale-Ryser realizability + binding/schema margin consts + incidence-built fixtures + real-producer positive control} ∪ {identity_policy/neutral_fill_note const-pin + identity triangulation} ∪ {exact built-in container types + str-key guards on every dict + untrusted-value-free diagnostics + numeric conversion overflow containment}. Boundary held: no provider/live/network/DataHub/production/ship-gate, no Cut 6-b~6-g, no scoring-weight change, no A-share/A-long/US-long. - **Codex slice-1a review FAIL (2026-06-30; `R-USSHORT-BATCH5-STATUS-RECORD-ROW-BINDING-GAP`)**: the new offline status-source layer is not ready to close R1 remainder (a). **Scope reviewed**: `engine/us_short_status_source.py`, `tests/provider/test_us_short_status_source.py`, `schemas/us_short_batch5_status_source_access_packet.schema.json`, `docs/us_short_batch5_status_source_access_packet_20260630.json`, and the adjacent `cheap_eligible` row-consumer boundary. This did not review or run provider/live status calls, `runners/us_short_universe_fetch.py` rewiring, candidate-artifact status-provenance schema expansion, R3 seam, DataHub, production, broker/order automation, A-share, A-long, or US-long. **Technical finding**: `status_flags_for_row(record)` validates only the status-record shape and then returns bare `row_flags` detached from `record["ticker"]`; it has no row-ticker parameter or identity check. Direct probe resolved a clean AAPL status record, extracted `{'delisted': False, 'halted': False, 'bankruptcy': False, 'otc': False}`, merged those flags into a separate DEAD row, and `cheap_eligible` returned `{'ticker': 'DEAD', 'eligible': True, 'reasons': []}` even though the same reference payload marked DEAD inactive/delisted. Same-class provenance probe mutated a resolved record to top-level `ticker='000001.SZ'`, `flags.delisted.as_of='1900-01-01'`, and `flags.delisted.coverage='nonsense'`; `validate_status_record` still returned `True` and `status_flags_for_row` still emitted all-clean row flags. The existing focused tests pass because they cover minimal/missing/wrong-source/bankruptcy-None shape mutants, but not record-to-row identity binding, canonical ticker validation, per-flag `as_of`/`observed_at` equality, coverage enums, or source-specific provenance semantics. **Why material**: this layer is explicitly intended to convert real per-ticker status-source observations into the `cheap_eligible` row contract before the runner removes its hardwired `False` status flags. If the API can detach a clean status record from its ticker, a caller/seam bug or forged intermediate record can turn "real status checked" into another hardwired-clean path for a different ticker, reintroducing the R1 fail-open class before live integration exists. The blast radius is bounded today because the runner is not rewired and live fetch is still gated, but the offline integration surface would be unsafe to consume as-is. **Required repair**: bind status records to rows at the conversion boundary and harden record validation. A safe shape is `status_flags_for_row(record, *, row_ticker)` or equivalent, requiring canonical `record["ticker"] == canonical_us_ticker(row_ticker)` before any flags are emitted; noncanonical record tickers must be rejected by `validate_status_record`; each flag's provenance `as_of`/`observed_at` must match the top-level record, and `coverage` / source-specific provenance values must be closed-world validated according to the resolver's emitted states. Add adversarial tests for AAPL-clean-record applied to DEAD row rejected/no flags, noncanonical top-level ticker rejected, mismatched per-flag as_of/observed_at rejected, invalid coverage rejected, and the positive same-ticker clean/delisted/halted/bankruptcy paths. Keep the repair narrow: no provider call, no runner rewire, no candidate-artifact schema relaxation, no R3 seam/DataHub/production, no broker/order path, and no A-share crossing. **Verification this review**: direct identity/provenance probes reproduced the fail-open; existing focused status/access tests ran `60 OK`; full offline `python -m unittest discover tests -p '*us_short*'` ran `2826 OK`; doc-route guards ran `52 OK`; `py_compile engine/us_short_status_source.py` OK; `git diff --check` warning-only CRLF. No network/provider/live/DataHub/production execution was run.

**(F)** source-specific value TYPES (`reference_active_value` bool|None / `primary_exchange_value` nonempty-str|None / `halt_feed_observed` strict bool / `lookback_window` str|None / `filing_accession_if_found`); OUTPUT MATRIX = `resolve_status_record`(producer) → `validate_status_record`(validator) → `status_flags_for_row`(conversion boundary → cheap_eligible); no schema/render/CLI exit (the candidate-artifact status-provenance schema is 1b). **Tests** (`tests/provider/test_us_short_status_source.py::RecordRowBindingTest`, 19): record↔other-row rejected, noncanonical + lowercase top ticker rejected, per-flag as_of + observed_at mismatch (all 4 flags), invalid coverage (all 4), value/coverage combo forgeries, source-specific bad types, bankruptcy value↔screen_status + found↔accession, halted observed↔value, noncanonical row_ticker rejected, + 7 positive resolver-output controls proving no over-constraint. If its refs, source-to-flag mapping, and timestamp can drift while schema-valid, a future LLM can be misrouted even though the false authorization booleans remain pinned. Add same-shape mutants listed above, all must error while the real artifact remains valid. A list from seven calendar days earlier cannot establish that a symbol is not halted at the current PIT. The single shared clock also cannot represent independently fresh ticker-reference and halt-feed observations, so a later live producer cannot fail one stale critical source to unknown without discarding or falsely refreshing the others. **Materiality**: `halted` is a conservative-reject critical flag. This path can turn stale absence into a clean status and admit a currently halted ticker while the record and consumer validator both report valid provenance. Green tests demonstrate only consistency with the blanket policy, not closure against the owner contract. **Required repair**: make critical-source freshness/effective time source-specific, or enforce a stricter offline rule that stale/undated `exchange_halt_feed` evidence remains unknown/rejected and can never emit `halted=False`. The halt-feed clean path must be bound to the decision PIT/prior settled session required by the binding; ticker-reference freshness may use a separately reviewed policy. Represent independent source observation/effective clocks in the input/provenance contract rather than copying one record-level timestamp to every flag. Add reverse tests showing a seven-day-old empty halt list cannot clear `halted`, while a decision-PIT/prior-settled-session feed can; retain Repair 5's ET normalization, future rejection, dated-packet pin, and valid weekend/holiday handling. - **Repair 6 + clean-slate full re-review closure (Codex 2026-07-01; PASS; committed `4351ebc8`; OFFLINE Cut 1a only)**: user explicitly authorized Codex to repair and then fully re-review 1a. **Root cause**: the prior implementation modeled freshness as one record-level seven-calendar-day date window, so it could not distinguish independently stale ticker-reference, halt-feed, and SEC-screen observations; it also checked only the ET date, not the owner design's legal run window. This defect pre-dated Repair 5; Repair 5 exposed it by replacing unlimited staleness with a blanket seven-day policy, but did not create the underlying shared-clock problem. **Repair**: `engine/us_short_status_source.py` now derives the exact offline decision window from the frozen NYSE/NASDAQ calendar (`presets/us_short_market_calendar_2026_2027.json`): record `observed_at` must be in `[prior settled session close, decision-session 09:30 ET open)`, including DST, holidays, and 13:00 ET half-days. Every observed source payload carries its own RFC3339 `observed_at`, cannot be later than the record clock, and is independently checked against that window. Stale ticker-reference and halt-feed observations emit `None/coverage=stale`; `status_flags_for_row` omits those flags, so `cheap_eligible` conservatively rejects instead of receiving clean `False`. Source clocks remain separate in per-flag provenance; delisted/otc share only their common ticker-reference clock. A stale SEC negative screen becomes unscreened/stale, while a stale, accession-backed `bankrupt_8k_found` remains a disqualifying `stale_positive` so freshness cannot erase durable positive evidence. An observed SEC screen now requires a nonblank `lookback_window`, preventing empty-window `screened_no_filing` provenance. ### R-USSHORT-BATCH2-PRICE-NAN-IMPLICIT-FAILCLOSE-FRAGILE - price_engine NaN inputs are only ACCIDENTALLY fail-closed (no explicit isfinite guard)

- Status: **open** (deferred P3 hygiene; user-acknowledged 2026-06-22 — NOT a blocking fix; registered so it is not lost on the desktop review `cc_review_v2.md`).
- Severity: **P3** (robustness hygiene; currently SAFE by accident, not a fail-open — fragility risk only).
- Source: `cc_review_v2.md` §5.9 — the converged batch-2 review's Round C / OPTIONAL-hygiene list (explicitly NOT a Round A/B Required; the review marked Round C "不挡 Round A / 可进批3").
- Technical finding: `engine/us_short_price_engine.py` does not explicitly guard NaN in its numeric price inputs (close / ATR / support / resistance). A NaN currently fails closed only ACCIDENTALLY — e.g. a NaN risk is caught by the `risk > 0 else 0.0` path, and `effective_support` / `effective_resistance` can return a NaN value tagged `'strong'`. It works today, but the safety rests on an IMPLICIT side-effect, not an explicit `math.isfinite` check. (Line refs in cc_review_v2 predate Round A/B; price_engine has since changed — malformed-input sweep `18cd7494`, de-spike `4c6c31d3` — so re-verify exact lines at fix time.)
- Why deferred / low: no money/safety fail-open in normal operation (real inputs are finite price floats; the accidental guard holds). The fragility is that a future refactor (batch-3+ touching price_engine) could silently break the implicit NaN-safety.
- Disposition: add explicit `math.isfinite` guards on the price numeric inputs (NaN/Inf → degrade-to-observe / None, like the other engines' strict `_finite_number`). Best done WHEN batch-3 next touches price_engine, OR in a dedicated Round C hygiene slice. Not started.
- Closure: a `修复` slice adds the explicit finite guards + adversarial NaN/Inf tests across support/resistance/RR, Codex re-`审查 PASS`, 用户 `提交`. Until then this stays a tracked deferred P3 (no behavior change required now).

### R-USSHORT-BATCH2-MACROCLUSTER-ELEVATED-EFFECTS-NOT-CONST-PINNED - macro_cluster gives `elevated` a risk_tag/banner that lives only in code, not in the governance preset

- Status: **open** (deferred P3 hygiene; user-acknowledged 2026-06-22 — NOT a blocking fix; registered so it is not lost on the desktop review `cc_review_v2.md`).
- Severity: **P3** (governance source-of-truth gap; strictly conservative, NOT a fail-open).
- Source: `cc_review_v2.md` §4.5 — the converged batch-2 review's Round C / OPTIONAL-hygiene list (NOT a Round A/B Required).
- Technical finding: `engine/us_short_macro_cluster.py` emits `risk_tag=True` / `report_banner=True` for the `elevated` warning level, but design §8 (≈line 229) + the `us_short_macro_cluster` governance preset only pin `high_warning_effects` (the `high` level). So the `elevated` effects are a rule that lives ONLY in engine code, not in the const-pinned governance — a source-of-truth gap. Strictly conservative (no size effect, hard_cap always False), so NOT a fail-open. (Re-verify exact lines at fix time.)
- Why deferred / low: not a behavior risk (`elevated` only tags/banners, no sizing/veto power). The gap is governance drift — if someone edits the elevated behavior, no schema/triangulation guard catches it (only the engine test does).
- Disposition: EITHER pin an `elevated_effects` block in `presets/us_short_macro_cluster_governance_*.json` (+ schema const-pin + a triangulation test schema==preset==engine), OR add a one-line design §8 note that `elevated` carries a soft tag/banner. The const-pin option is the more rigorous governance fix. Best done WHEN batch-3 wires the field_registry / no-dangling governance layer, OR in a dedicated Round C slice. Not started. (Touches the frozen preset/schema or design — needs the usual care + user nod.)
- Closure: a `修复` slice pins the elevated effects (preset + schema + triangulation) or adds the design note, Codex re-`审查 PASS`, 用户 `提交`. Until then this stays a tracked deferred P3.

### SR-DATA-004 - Suspend daily completeness threshold may false-fail on large-scale suspension days

- Severity: P3
- Status: open
- Owner phase: A-short weekly operation / Phase 6b maintenance
- Evidence: `SR-DATA-001` resolved the partial-`daily` silent drop path by requiring non-empty `pro.daily` payload coverage to meet `suspend_daily_min_coverage = 0.95`. Claude review accepted the fail-safe behavior but noted the threshold assumes normal-day suspensions stay below 5%; rare market-stress days with unusually broad suspensions could push true traded-code coverage below 95% and abort the run even when the provider response is complete.
- Accepted calibration: this is a low-risk operational watch item, not evidence that normal weekly runs are broken. A clear abort is safer than silently inferring missing rows as suspended stocks, and `suspend_daily_min_coverage` is already a configurable threshold.
- Mitigation evidence: the current observability slice makes `A-EGS/egs_main.py:get_suspend_info` write `logs/suspend_daily_coverage_<asof>.json` on pass, low-coverage fail, cache-hit, and no-daily skip paths; schema-validated `data_health` v1.2.0 `metrics.suspend_daily_coverage` mirrors the same latest observation. This does not close the watch item because no real weekly run has been reviewed yet.
- Required next action: monitor the first real weekly runs' `logs/suspend_daily_coverage_<asof>.json` and `data_health.metrics.suspend_daily_coverage`; if legitimate market-wide suspension conditions trip the threshold, tune the threshold or add a reviewed calendar / incident override with a focused regression test.

### SR-SKILL-002 - A-short frozen v14.2 reference can still be pasted as a runtime prompt

- Severity: P2
- Status: blocked
- Owner phase: A-short Skill / reference hygiene
- Evidence: `skills/a_short_analysis/reference/v14.2_spec.md` opens with imperative persona / full-framework execution / operation-advice language, but `CLAUDE.md` explicitly says `skills/a_short_analysis/reference/v14.2_spec.md` must not be edited because the design is frozen.
- Accepted calibration: this is partially mitigated by `skills/a_short_analysis/SKILL.md`, which is the active Skill entrypoint and now states that `v14.2_spec.md` is a frozen design spec, not a runtime prompt, and cannot authorize live operation advice / buy-sell actions / sizing outside the schema-validated runner and reviewed ship-gate evidence. Residual risk remains if someone copies the frozen reference file alone without the Skill boundary.
- Required next action: do not edit `skills/a_short_analysis/reference/v14.2_spec.md` unless the user explicitly lifts the freeze for a non-design banner only. Until then, treat direct-pasted v14.2 reference text as unsafe / non-authoritative for runtime advice and route through the Skill plus schema-validated runner boundary.
- Mitigation evidence: this repair strengthens `skills/a_short_analysis/SKILL.md` with a `Non-Runtime Reference Boundary` section and keeps the frozen reference file unchanged.
- Verification: `rg -n "Non-Runtime Reference Boundary|live operation advice|SR-SKILL-002|v14.2_spec.md" skills\a_short_analysis\SKILL.md docs\system_risk_register.md CLAUDE.md` confirms the boundary, residual-risk entry, and frozen-spec constraint.

### R-USSHORT-FORWARD-LIFECYCLE-MERGER-TICKER-SEMANTICS-UNRESOLVED - inactive status cannot safely identify a merger or successor ticker

- Severity: P1
- Status: open
- Owner phase: US-short active-only forward evidence / lifecycle containment
- Evidence: `runners/us_short_universe_fetch.py` already binds active-listing, halt, OTC, and bankruptcy status provenance into private candidate artifacts. The forward lifecycle slice adds `runners/us_short_forward_lifecycle_capture.py` and `schemas/us_short_forward_lifecycle_observation.schema.json`: a frozen private active-universe snapshot is compared only with a later private status-sourced candidate artifact. It retains the original frozen symbols and records `inactive_or_ticker_change_unresolved`, `halted`, `bankruptcy`, `otc_or_exchange_migration`, missing-current-row, or critical-status-unknown events as private manual-review/new-entry blocks. The disposition slice adds `engine/us_short_corporate_action_disposition.py` and `schemas/us_short_corporate_action_manual_disposition.schema.json`: only a separately source-bound, manually confirmed event can produce an exact stock/cash/manual-exit ticket. The offline identity/boundary slice adds CIK plus controlled share-class identity, a one-ticker manual-review freeze for source failure, and disabled yfinance/SEC interface skeletons. The manual event recorder transforms an explicitly confirmed human SEC transcription into the planner's exact event, accepts an EDGAR URL only when its CIK binds to the old or identity-bound successor security, and hashes rather than stores the URL; CVR and invalid/inconsistent input remain one-ticker review freezes. With the user-authorized account-state wiring, its runner reads a private local `us_short_account_state` only after explicit `--confirm-account-read`, extracts only the old ticker's validated long shares, emits only a digest binding, and writes the actual ticket only to a gitignored/external private path. The source-intake slice adds a default-dry-run, one-document SEC 8-K/8-K-A strict parser that emits only an unconfirmed candidate, plus a one-ticker yfinance split/dividend/source-availability alarm; complex/ambiguous SEC terms and any low-trust yfinance problem remain ticker-scoped review outcomes. The offline workflow slice adds an identity-bound, schema-validated one-command chain over lifecycle, SEC/yfinance, optional Massive diagnostic, the existing manual recorder, validated private account-state read, and the existing disposition planner. It emits a private ticket only after explicit human confirmation, requires a strict SEC candidate to match the confirmed event exactly, hashes the exact persisted ticket bytes, cleans partial outputs, and const-pins every automatic/paper/ship authority false. This implementation performed no live call, read no real account/raw artifact, confirms no SEC semantics, and changes no selection.
- Accepted calibration: an inactive listing or an absent current candidate can indicate delisting, ticker change, merger, acquisition, source outage, or candidate-artifact corruption. It is therefore a fail-closed trigger, not proof of a merger, a successor security, exchange ratio, cash consideration, effective date, or a tradeable exit price.
- Required next action: a strict parser candidate still requires a human to verify the specific SEC source and explicitly confirm old/successor security, effective date, stock/cash terms, and failure/unknown policy through the confirmed-event recorder; DEFM14A/CVR/complex or ambiguous terms remain fully manual. The currently available offline/manual engineering chain ends at a private disposition ticket. Any attempt to infer complex semantics, reconcile tolerances, or set the three §12.1 paper confirmations requires a separate reviewed contract and authorization. The authorized local account read remains read-only and private. No auto conversion, cash booking, forced exit, return calculation, selection/ranking change, DataHub, production, or ship-gate claim is allowed.
- Verification: `tests.provider.test_us_short_forward_lifecycle_capture` covers frozen-symbol retention plus inactive/ticker ambiguity, halt, bankruptcy, OTC, missing-row, unknown, malformed provenance, canonical-ticker, confirmation, and no-partial-write paths. `tests.test_us_short_corporate_action_disposition` covers all four event shapes, exact fractional stock entitlement, cents cash entitlement, source-confirmation/ticker/ratio/forced-exit rejection, and boundary-tamper rejection. `tests.test_us_short_corporate_action_event_recorder` covers account-state-only cash/stock/forced round trips, binding mismatch/no-position/malformed/nonlong rejection, confirmation, CVR/invalid freeze, identity binding, and old-or-successor SEC-URL CIK checks; its runner/schema suites pin opt-in account reading, private-ticket output, no account leakage, and confirmed/manual-review boundaries. The source-intake engine/runner/schema suites cover strict cash/stock/combined extraction, successor-filed identity binding, DEFM14A/CVR/adjustment/election/multi-value freeze, one-call authorization/URL/chronology preflight, no raw/URL/User-Agent output, yfinance clear/split/dividend/missing/mismatch/malformed neutralization, and const-false authority claims. The workflow engine/runner/schema suites cover evidence/identity/ticker/event binding, explicit confirmation/account-read gates, strict-candidate mismatch rejection, private-path/no-overwrite handling, exact ticket-byte digest binding, partial-output cleanup, forged authority rejection, and permanently blocked paper confirmations.
- Plain language: 股票从新名单消失时，系统会停下并要求人工核对；它不会擅自猜成并购、换成新股票、算现金，或把旧记录删掉。
